# Chat service test cleanup audit

Audited all **632 baseline tests** in **35 files**, including the production owner of each. The inventory also contains 33 `describe` containers, which are grouping rather than additional tests. This ledger records baseline names even when a declaration was deleted. Read every test and its owner before assigning the outcome; assertion excerpts below record evidence rather than classify tests by a pattern.

Baseline dispositions: **DELETE 16**, **REWRITE 40**, **OPTIMIZE 2**, **CONSOLIDATE 17**, **KEEP 557**. **25 test declarations removed**; remaining 607. Test-only changes: **+207/−502 lines, net -295**. CONSOLIDATE includes both removed duplicates and surviving owners, so it is not a deletion count.

## Contract and risk method

For every retained row the six requirements are supplied jointly by its file rationale and its case-specific fixed assertions: (1) the cited independent contract, (2) the named credible caller-visible failure, (3) literal fixture/status/state/bytes rather than production-derived answers, (4) the named public or stable policy/protocol/storage seam, (5) behavior and persisted/wire outputs rather than source spelling/call order, and (6) the distinct owner/scenario versus stronger tests. A pure policy function is retained only where its output is a production contract and these examples enumerate credible priority, boundary, safety or temporal failures; branch execution alone is not an oracle.

Fakes isolate a host process, filesystem-independent account preparation, transport loss or time. They do not calculate the behavior being asserted. Integration tests use actual daemon orchestration, real files and/or real local HTTP streams where those boundaries are part of the contract. Refactor-preserving identity comparisons assert consistency across public request/response/storage boundaries, never the implementation hash used to generate an ID.

Unchanged tests are KEEP only after review, not because they passed. Case names describe the specific scenario/failure; the oracle excerpt gives an independently checkable expected outcome. For loops within one declaration, the complete test remains the authoritative matrix. Shared file-level rationale explains why another layer does not supersede it. Risk is high for auth/privacy/storage/recovery removals; none of those unique owners is deleted. Deletion risk is confined to duplicate owners, declaration/log-copy inventories and unsupported private manipulation.

## Changes and removed support

- Removed duplicate source/declaration/header/catalog inventories and exact human log-copy tests. Security headers remain asserted on actual signed-page HTTP responses; live gzip flush remains asserted through a streaming proxy response.
- Consolidated normal-shell classification into an existing mixed-session reattach and generic command refusal into the existing real outbox error matrix. Raw owner schema tests remain in packages/config; HTTP normalization/accepted maximums remain here.
- Repaired false-positive idle-compaction negatives: disabled settings, foreign providers and pending guards now run inside the valid compaction window. Their old expiry-time checks could pass because the cache had expired even if the guarded policy was broken.
- Replaced private map/cache synchronization or mutation with public recheck, injected time, fresh service/adoption from schema-decoded persisted records, real filesystem journal notification, real unreadable-store failure and actual current-runtime Admin calls.
- Removed implementation-derived fork/cascade/idle command-ID expectations. Public idempotency, request correlation, once-only effects and conflict behavior remain.
- Replaced 31 MiB of generated/written zero-filled oversized-image fixtures with actual sparse files of the same sizes. The real 10 MiB per-image and 25 MiB aggregate checks still run.
- Narrowed instruction tests to independently fixed UTF-8 byte budgets, tool/collection references, sanitation and omission semantics. Removed exact production-constant concatenation and incidental clipping/maximal-fit expectations.

Private-only production exports removed after repository-wide caller checks: `cascadeCommandId`, `delegateDescendants`, `delegateUnfinished`, `idleCompactionCommandId`, `isImageBytes`, `waitResponseOf`, `forkIdFor`, `WORKFLOW_GRANT_NOTICE`, `knowledgeLine`, `ORQUESTER_INSTRUCTIONS_FIXED`, `WORKFLOW_OWNED_LINE`, `LEGACY_CHAT_TOOL_ALIASES`, `LegacyChatToolAlias`, `aliasTool`, `advertisedChatTools`, `alwaysAdmin`, `DAEMON_POINTING_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MANIFEST_SESSIONS_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`. Their existing internal implementations remain. The unused legacy alias-name inventory export `LEGACY_CHAT_TOOL_NAMES` is removed entirely. The MCP owner removes the only external constant-derived workflow notice assertion. No production behavior is changed.

No snapshots, fixture captures, dependencies or package configurations were removed in this scope: none became unused through these deletions. Temporary fixture construction and dead helper imports were removed with their tests. `openInsideRoot` remains because its deterministic race/security checks uniquely require the actual post-realpath file-open boundary; adding a production injection hook solely to reach it through publish would increase coupling.

## Performance and validation

Root owns the one-run-per-worktree schedule and repository-wide measurements. No scope-local suite or typecheck has been run yet. Do not treat syntax parsing or source review as executed regression tests. Before/final overall wall clock, comparable slowest-file timings, variance, absolute/percentage improvement and final gates must be filled from root-run results; no CI speedup is inferred from deleted lines.

Observed baseline individual timings in root's cache-disabled, four-file-concurrency run: actual over-cap 500 MiB MCP upload **2010.260 ms**; first box service fixture **1127.890 ms**; first agent-tools route fixture **1116.890 ms**; first service launch **1015.460 ms**; first profile launch **851.870 ms**; owner create HTTP **662.700 ms**; aggregate-image refusal **125.458 ms**, image-cap refusal **102.040 ms**; serialized mixed-session writes **111.244 ms**. These are individual observations, not comparable total-suite speedups. First-case cold module/native startup contributes materially; the upload intentionally streams a true public-cap violation.

Expensive retained contracts: the real 500 MiB upload uniquely proves the daemon stream cap/ownership/413 behavior (MCP tests own only mapping; host-client tests own causal errors); local Unix HTTP service/profile/box launch cases protect account, persistence and host-protocol composition; real image aggregate/security checks retain actual file access. No arbitrary hard time limit, timeout increase, retry or gate exclusion is introduced.

Checks completed before execution scheduling: TypeScript syntax parsing of the prepared source/test files; repository-wide searches for removed exported callers; owned `git diff --check` (passed). Root will execute the focused daemon targets and required typechecks/full gates. Baseline failures, final outcome and mutation/regression probes are recorded below once available.

Focused command (run from apps/daemon under root scheduling): `TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1 node --import tsx --test --test-concurrency=4` followed by the changed test paths relative to that package. Root also runs `pnpm check:changed`, `pnpm check` and `pnpm test` as feasible.

### `apps/daemon/src/agent-chat/delegates.test.ts`

**Independent source:** unified MCP design 2026-10-15 §§8,13; GUI stop/delegation behavior. **Stable seam/non-test consumers:** DelegateStopCascade commandAccepted/stop lifecycle over fixed lineage records and fake daemon protocol recording literal interrupt recipients.

**Real failures and unique ownership:** The cascade owns descendant cancellation, depth/fork/opened exclusions and deduplication. Adapter interrupt tests own one provider command, not daemon lineage traversal. Private walk/hash-only tests are removed.

**Oracle/refactor/risk:** Each row below records concrete inputs/scenario in its baseline name and independent expected outputs in its assertions. ### `apps/daemon/src/agent-chat/lineage-routes.test.ts`

**Independent source:** visual/session-tools design §6 lineage; unified MCP §§5,8 caller principals. **Stable seam/non-test consumers:** Registered Fastify lineage HTTP routes using actual principal prehandler boundary.

**Real failures and unique ownership:** Lineage service owns merge persistence; these cases independently own person/agent authorization, self-only restrictions, request normalization and HTTP error mapping. Fakes return domain outcomes without implementing route validation.

**Oracle/refactor/risk:** Each row below records concrete inputs/scenario in its baseline name and independent expected outputs in its assertions. These are production-consumed behavior or protocol/storage/security boundaries; behavior-preserving internal renames/reordering leave them valid. ### `apps/daemon/src/agent-chat/lineage.test.ts`

**Independent source:** visual/session-tools design §6; unified MCP §§8,13; durable merge outbox and command receipts. **Stable seam/non-test consumers:** LineageService public merge/whenIdle/read APIs with actual on-disk outbox, fixed session registry and fake host protocol.

**Real failures and unique ownership:** Host receipts prevent one command executing twice; this daemon owner must persist/restore pending merges, correlate turn text and errors, preserve caller IDs and publish lineage. Route tests do not execute the merge outbox. Duplicate generic refusal lifecycle is consolidated into the error matrix.

**Oracle/refactor/risk:** Each row below records concrete inputs/scenario in its baseline name and independent expected outputs in its assertions. These are production-consumed behavior or protocol/storage/security boundaries; behavior-preserving internal renames/reordering leave them valid. | --- | --- | --- |

| 11: gzip is accepted by name or wildcard, never at q=0 | KEEP | Protects gzip is accepted by name or wildcard, never at q=0. Oracle: assert.equal(acceptsGzip(undefined), false); assert.equal(acceptsGzip(""), false); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("identity"), false); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("gzip, deflate, br, zstd"), true, "a browser's fetch"); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("GZIP"), true); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("x-gzip"), true); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("br;q=1.0, gzip;q=0.5"), true); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("gzip;q=0"), false); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("gzip;q=0, *"), false, "an explicit refusal beats the wildcard"); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("*"), true); assert.equal(acceptsGzip("*;q=0"), false); assert.equal(acceptsGzip(["deflate", "gzip"]), true) |

Oracle: assert.equal(forwarder.heartbeatIfIdle(2_500), false, "not idle long enough yet"); assert.equal(forwarder.heartbeatIfIdle(2_500), true, "idle at a line start"); assert.equal(forwarder.heartbeatIfIdle(2_500), false, "a comment mid-line would corrupt it"); assert.equal(forwarder.heartbeatIfIdle(2_500), false, "bytes just flowed"); assert.equal(forwarder.heartbeatIfIdle(2_500), true); assert.equal(output(), ':hb\n{"kind":"snapshot"}\n:hb\n'); assert.equal(forwarder.heartbeatIfIdle(0), false, "nothing after the end") |

### `apps/daemon/src/agent-chat/replies.test.ts`

**Independent source:** unified MCP design §§8,13 reply subscriptions, exchanges, crash recovery, hop/consumer limits; GUI stop safety. **Stable seam/non-test consumers:** ReplyRouter public subscribe/wait/release/drain/recheck APIs, durable storage, fixed session registry and host receipts/history boundary.

**Real failures and unique ownership:** Lineage merge tests own explicit merges; waits owns only primitive waiter bookkeeping. These scenarios independently own target/turn binding, once-only routing/wait consumption, restart crash windows, stale history, exchanges, closure and stop suppression. Private hand/claim/cache tests are removed or rewritten through public rescan/restart seams.

**Oracle/refactor/risk:** Each row below records concrete inputs/scenario in its baseline name and independent expected outputs in its assertions. These are production-consumed behavior or protocol/storage/security boundaries; behavior-preserving internal renames/reordering leave them valid. Existing fakes supply inputs/transport outcomes, not expected product decisions. Surviving rows are the lowest stable owner for the listed failure. Relevant validation is this file plus the package typecheck and affected integration owners named above.

| Baseline line and exact test name | Disposition | Failure, independent oracle and surviving protection |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 506: persists the subscription BEFORE the message is posted, and binds the turn it caused | KEEP | Protects persists the subscription BEFORE the message is posted, and binds the turn it caused. Oracle: assert.equal(res.delivery, "sent"); assert.equal(res.commandId, "id-2"); assert.ok(disk, "the target's /turn came after the write"); assert.deepEqual(disk.subscriptions.map((s) => [s.id, s.state, s.commandId]), [[res.subscriptionId, "notify", res.commandId]]); assert.equal(post.body.commandId, res.commandId, "posted under the subscription's fixed id"); assert.deepEqual(post.body.causedBy, exchange(3), "the caused turn carries its exchange"); assert.equal((post.body.context as Array<{ kind: string }>)[0]!.kind, THREAD_MESSAGE_CONTEXT_KIND); assert.equal(sub.posted?.messageId, "msg-1", "bound by the receipt's events to the user message it opened") |

### `apps/daemon/src/agent-chat/secret-requests.test.ts`

| 41: hands the value to the waiting call, once, and publishes only the request | KEEP | Protects hands the value to the waiting call, once, and publishes only the request. Oracle: assert.deepEqual(requests.list("t1"), [request]); assert.deepEqual(requests.list("other"), []); assert.equal(await requests.answer("t1", request.requestId, { answer: "provide", value: VALUE }), "provided"); assert.deepEqual(await answer, { outcome: "provided", value: VALUE }); assert.equal(await requests.answer("t1", request.requestId, { answer: "provide", value: "again" }), null); assert.deepEqual(requests.list("t1"), []); assert.deepEqual( events.map((event) => [event.open, event.outcome]), [ [true, undefined], [false, "provided"] ] ); assert.ok(!JSON.stringify(events).includes(VALUE), "the bus never carries the value"); assert.deepEqual(events[0]?.request, { requestId: "req-1", sessionId: "t1", name: "API_KEY", prompt: "Paste your API key.", purpose: "Call the API", createdAt: events[0]?.request.createdAt }) |

### `apps/daemon/src/agent-chat/service.test.ts`

**Independent source:** GUI design §§3.1,3.4,6.1–6.3; visual/session-tools §6; unified MCP §§5,8,13; AGENTS append-only/idempotency/account rules. **Stable seam/non-test consumers:** AgentChatService public create/switch/fork/reconcile/upload/publish and routes against a real local Unix HTTP host peer.

**Real failures and unique ownership:** ChatSessionManager owns raw tab persistence; host owns provider execution. These tests uniquely protect daemon launch composition, request-before/after state transitions, host unknown outcomes, fork command conflicts/recovery and actual upload cap/lifecycle. Expected generated IDs now come from public request/response correlation, not the production hash function.

**Oracle/refactor/risk:** Each row below records concrete inputs/scenario in its baseline name and independent expected outputs in its assertions. These are production-consumed behavior or protocol/storage/security boundaries; behavior-preserving internal renames/reordering leave them valid. Existing fakes supply inputs/transport outcomes, not expected product decisions. Surviving rows are the lowest stable owner for the listed failure. Relevant validation is this file plus the package typecheck and affected integration owners named above.

| Baseline line and exact test name | Disposition | Failure, independent oracle and surviving protection |
| --- | --- | --- |

### `apps/daemon/src/agent-chat/session-index.test.ts`

### `apps/daemon/src/agent-chat/tool-approvals.test.ts`

**Independent source:** unified MCP §§7,13 approval first-wins, privacy, pending cap and restart cancellation; preview limits and person-only principal contract. **Stable seam/non-test consumers:** ToolApprovalRegistry public open/list/detail/answer/recover/shutdown plus actual Fastify route principal boundary and real journal.

**Real failures and unique ownership:** Approval port enriches cards and persists grants; it cannot prove registry deadlines/CAS/cancellation/summary redaction/durable cancellation. Filesystem notification waits observe durable journal completion without private synchronization state.

**Oracle/refactor/risk:** Each row below records concrete inputs/scenario in its baseline name and independent expected outputs in its assertions. These are production-consumed behavior or protocol/storage/security boundaries; behavior-preserving internal renames/reordering leave them valid. Existing fakes supply inputs/transport outcomes, not expected product decisions. Surviving rows are the lowest stable owner for the listed failure.
